Well, um... I don't want to sound ungrateful, because I really do have a wonderful life, and I know I have so much to be thankful for. But... I guess, yes, I have been feeling a little down.

Well, um... I guess I wouldn't use the word "depressed," exactly. That sounds so... so severe, doesn't it? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying all day, because I'm definitely not. I still go to work, I still do my job as an appraiser, and I keep the house running. 

But... if I'm being completely truthful, which I know is what you need... I suppose most days I do feel just a little... down. It's almost like there's this thin gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being so kind, or like we talked about, when we're all cooking together. I can go through all the motions and smile, but I just don't feel that brightness I used to. I feel terrible admitting that, like I'm not appreciating my life. I really hope I don't sound ungrateful, Doctor. Is it normal to feel that way sometimes?

I don't just sit around doing nothing, I promise. I try very hard to stay active and keep up with everything. It's just that the joy I used to get from it all... it's just not as strong as it should be. I hope that's okay to say. I'm not trying to be negative, Doctor, really I'm not. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I have trouble concentrating. I mean, my job is really detailed. Looking over claims, you have to catch every little thing, and I'm very careful about that. I always double-check my work to make sure it's right.

I don't just sit around doing nothing, I promise. I try very hard to stay active and keep up with everything. It's just that the joy I used to get from it all... it's just not as strong as it should be. I hope that's okay to say. I'm not trying to be negative, Doctor, really I'm not. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I feel like I might be frustrating you, and I really don't mean to. I just... I really wouldn't say I've been feeling down or less interested in things for two weeks in a row.

Is it normal to feel that way sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, or do you find it hard to sleep even when you're tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I eat pretty normally, I think. I try to make sure my husband and I have balanced meals, so I haven't really noticed any big changes there. And my weight has been pretty steady, thankfully. I know at my age that can be a struggle, but I've been lucky. \n\nAs for sleep... I usually sleep fine. Sometimes I might toss and turn a little bit if I'm thinking about work—you know how claims can get complicated—but I wouldn't say it's hard to sleep. I really try to keep a good schedule so I'm rested for the day. I hope that's the kind of information you need? I don't want to leave anything out if it's important.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you're not good enough, even when there's no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I definitely try my hardest at everything I do, whether it's at work or at home. I don't really sit around feeling guilty, because I always make sure I'm doing my part, you know? \n\nSometimes I might wonder if I could have handled a claim a little better, or if I should have cooked a nicer dinner, but... I think that's just normal, right? Just wanting to be a good wife and a good employee. I certainly don't feel like I'm not good enough. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm being defensive, Doctor, I just... I try really hard to be a good person and do things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving more slowly than usual, or have trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still get up and get ready for work at the same pace I always do, and I'm definitely not fidgety or anything. I've always been pretty calm and collected, especially since my job requires a lot of focus. \n\nI try really hard to stay active and keep up with everything, so I don't think I'm slowing down. I mean, I'm 55, so maybe I'm not running marathons, but I certainly get around just fine. I hope that's what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers. I really don't feel like I'm moving any differently than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or thinking clearly lately, even when you're trying to focus, like when you're working on a claim or reading something?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I have trouble concentrating. I mean, my job is really detailed. Looking over claims, you have to catch every little thing, and I'm very careful about that. I always double-check my work to make sure it's right. \n\nI guess sometimes, if it's the end of a long day, I might have to read a sentence twice, but... that's just normal tiredness, isn't it? Everybody gets a little fatigued when they've been staring at a screen for hours. But as far as real trouble thinking clearly, no, I feel like I'm doing a good job staying on top of things. I always want to make sure I'm doing my best work. I hope that answers your question alright? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm slipping at my job or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, with a loss of interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy and feeling down, or is this the first time you've experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try my best to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose there are times when I'm just a little tired, and maybe I don't feel like doing as much on the weekends as I used to, but... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... I feel terrible that I keep giving you the exact same answer. You must think I'm not listening or something, but I promise I am. It's just... no, even looking at just this past week, I haven't had a few days in a row where I lost interest in things.

I don't want to leave anything out if it's important.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you're not good enough, even when there's no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I definitely try my hardest at everything I do, whether it's at work or at home. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm not taking up time someone else might need more, but I came in because I've just been feeling so tired lately. \n\nI know everyone gets worn out, right? But it's getting to where, by midmorning at work... even if I've just been sitting and reviewing some standard files, or maybe walking a junior adjuster through a claim... it feels like my body and my brain are already running on empty. I'm really trying my best to push through it so I don't let my team down, but it's been difficult. Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I made an appointment for this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy along with the fatigue?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want to sound ungrateful, because I really do have a wonderful life, and I know I have so much to be thankful for. But... I guess, yes, I have been feeling a little down. \n\nIt's hard to explain without sounding like I'm complaining. It's almost like... like there's a gray film over everything most days. Even when my husband is being so sweet and kind, or when we're all cooking together with the family—which I usually love—I just sort of go through the motions. I smile and do my part, but I'm not really feeling the enjoyment I used to. \n\nI feel terrible even admitting that out loud. I really should be happier, shouldn't I? I hope it's okay that I'm sharing that with you, I just don't want you to think I'm not trying.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the times when you used to enjoy things—like cooking with your family—have you noticed that you don’t enjoy those things anymore, even when you’re trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd say I still enjoy them a little bit. I really try to, anyway. I mean, I love my family so much, and they're so good to me. I don't want to make it sound like I don't care about spending time with them, because I absolutely do. \n\nIt's just... maybe I'm just getting a little older, or I'm just tired from work, you know? I really am trying to appreciate the good things.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that activities like cooking with your family, which you used to enjoy, no longer give you pleasure—even when you’re making an effort to be involved?\n\nPatient: I, um... I feel like I might have just repeated myself a bit, didn't I? I'm so sorry if I'm not being clear. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nBut, yes, to answer your question... I guess it's like I said, I still try to be involved, and I don't want to say I get *no* pleasure at all from it, because that sounds so ungrateful. It's just... muted, maybe? Like I'm going through the steps, but the feeling isn't quite there. I'm really sorry if that's not the answer you were looking for. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you take less pleasure or interest in most activities—even ones you used to enjoy—most days?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd have to say yes, if I'm being completely honest. I really don't want to sound like a complainer, because I know people have it so much worse, but... yes. Most days, it just feels like it takes so much effort to be interested in things, even my favorite hobbies or... or just watching a movie with my husband. \n\nI still do them, of course! I don't just sit around doing nothing, I promise. I try very hard to stay active and keep up with everything. It's just that the joy I used to get from it all... it's just not as strong as it should be. I hope that's okay to say. I'm not trying to be negative, Doctor, really I'm not. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I wouldn't use the word \"depressed,\" exactly. That sounds so... so severe, doesn't it? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying all day, because I'm definitely not. I still go to work, I still do my job as an appraiser, and I keep the house running. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely truthful, which I know is what you need... I suppose most days I do feel just a little... down. It's almost like there's this thin gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being so kind, or like we talked about, when we're all cooking together. I can go through all the motions and smile, but I just don't feel that brightness I used to. I feel terrible admitting that, like I'm not appreciating my life. I really hope I don't sound ungrateful, Doctor. Is it normal to feel that way sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, or do you find it hard to sleep even when you're tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I eat pretty normally, I think. I try to make sure my husband and I have balanced meals, so I haven't really noticed any big changes there. And my weight has been pretty steady, thankfully. I know at my age that can be a struggle, but I've been lucky. \n\nAs for sleep... I usually sleep fine. Sometimes I might toss and turn a little bit if I'm thinking about work—you know how claims can get complicated—but I wouldn't say it's hard to sleep. I really try to keep a good schedule so I'm rested for the day. I hope that's the kind of information you need? I don't want to leave anything out if it's important.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty or like you're not good enough, even when there's no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I definitely try my hardest at everything I do, whether it's at work or at home. I don't really sit around feeling guilty, because I always make sure I'm doing my part, you know? \n\nSometimes I might wonder if I could have handled a claim a little better, or if I should have cooked a nicer dinner, but... I think that's just normal, right? Just wanting to be a good wife and a good employee. I certainly don't feel like I'm not good enough. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm being defensive, Doctor, I just... I try really hard to be a good person and do things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving more slowly than usual, or have trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still get up and get ready for work at the same pace I always do, and I'm definitely not fidgety or anything. I've always been pretty calm and collected, especially since my job requires a lot of focus. \n\nI try really hard to stay active and keep up with everything, so I don't think I'm slowing down. I mean, I'm 55, so maybe I'm not running marathons, but I certainly get around just fine. I hope that's what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers. I really don't feel like I'm moving any differently than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or thinking clearly lately, even when you're trying to focus, like when you're working on a claim or reading something?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I have trouble concentrating. I mean, my job is really detailed. Looking over claims, you have to catch every little thing, and I'm very careful about that. I always double-check my work to make sure it's right. \n\nI guess sometimes, if it's the end of a long day, I might have to read a sentence twice, but... that's just normal tiredness, isn't it? Everybody gets a little fatigued when they've been staring at a screen for hours. But as far as real trouble thinking clearly, no, I feel like I'm doing a good job staying on top of things. I always want to make sure I'm doing my best work. I hope that answers your question alright? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm slipping at my job or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, with a loss of interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy and feeling down, or is this the first time you've experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I wouldn't really say I've lost interest in things, or that I feel terribly down. I mean, I still go to work every day, and I still take care of the house. I try my best to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose there are times when I'm just a little tired, and maybe I don't feel like doing as much on the weekends as I used to, but... I think that's just a normal part of getting older, right? I certainly wouldn't call it a loss of pleasure. I still enjoy a quiet evening at home with my husband. I just don't want to make a big deal out of nothing. I hope that's the kind of information you're looking for? I really try to be a happy, productive person, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in the past where you felt down or lost interest in things for a couple of weeks or more, even before this current time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I've always tried to be the kind of person who just pushes through and gets things done, you know? \n\nI guess everyone has a bad week here and there where they feel a little stressed or tired, especially when work gets busy, but... I wouldn't say I've ever had a whole chunk of time where I was just down or not caring about things. I've always taken a lot of pride in being reliable for my family and my job. I really try not to let myself get into a slump like that. I hope I'm explaining that right? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just gives up on things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for two weeks or more, even before this current period?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know you just asked me something similar, and I really want to be helpful here, but... no, I haven't. I mean, I've always been a very steady person. \n\nI think sometimes people can let their emotions get the better of them, but I've always believed in just putting one foot in front of the other. Even if things are a little difficult, I just keep going. I wouldn't ever let myself get to a point where I just stopped caring for two whole weeks. That just doesn't sound like me at all. I really hope I'm giving you the right answers, Doctor. I just want to make sure you know I'm a very capable person.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm whether these feelings of being down and less interested in things have been happening most days and have lasted for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I feel like I might be frustrating you, and I really don't mean to. I just... I really wouldn't say I've been feeling down or less interested in things for two weeks in a row. \n\nI mean, I go to work, I do my job as a claims adjuster—which can be a bit demanding, you know—and I come home and take care of what needs to be done. I just don't think I've had a stretch like that where I'm just... not functioning or feeling low every single day. I really try to keep my chin up and just handle things as they come. I hope that makes sense? I just don't want you to think I'm falling apart or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times in the past two months when you felt less interest or pleasure in activities for several days in a row, even if it didn't last the whole two weeks?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I know you're trying to get a full picture, and I want to give you all the information you need, but... I just haven't had that. \n\nEven in the last couple of months, I've just been keeping up with my usual routine. I go to the office, I review the claims, I talk to the clients... I just do what I'm supposed to do. I don't really have time to sit around and feel uninterested in things, you know? I always try to find a little bit of satisfaction in a job well done, even if it's just a regular Tuesday. I really hope I'm being helpful by saying this. I just don't want to make it sound like I'm not engaged in my own life. I'm doing okay, really.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times in the past two weeks when you felt less interest or pleasure in activities for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm so sorry, I feel like a broken record, and I really hate to keep giving you the same answer. I know you're just doing your job thoroughly, and I appreciate that. But... no, even just looking at the past two weeks, I haven't had a stretch of days where I lost interest in things. \n\nI've just been doing my usual routine, keeping up with my claims at work and taking care of the house. I really do try to stay productive and keep busy. Am I answering these right for you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your evaluation, but I really haven't been letting things slide like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times in the past week when you felt less interest or pleasure in activities for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I feel terrible that I keep giving you the exact same answer. You must think I'm not listening or something, but I promise I am. It's just... no, even looking at just this past week, I haven't had a few days in a row where I lost interest in things. \n\nI've been keeping up with everything just fine. I actually closed a few tricky files at work this week, so I felt pretty good about getting those off my desk. I just... I really try to stay positive and keep moving forward. Is it alright that I don't have a different answer for you? I really want to make sure I'm doing this right and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there has been any time in the past year when you felt less interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy, even for just a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh my goodness, I really am starting to feel like I'm failing a test or something! I'm so sorry... even looking back over the whole past year, um, no. I really can't think of a time like that. \n\nI mean, it's been a busy year, sure, and work gets stressful with all the claim reviews and everything, but I've always managed to find some joy in my usual things. I don't just... check out and stop enjoying my life, I promise. I hope it's okay that my answer is still no. I really am racking my brain to make sure I give you the most accurate picture. Am I doing okay with all this?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there has been any time in the past year when you felt less interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy for at least two weeks, even if it was off and on?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel like I'm frustrating you with these answers, and I really, really don't want to do that. I know you're trying to find a pattern or something, and I appreciate how thorough you are. But... honestly, no. Not even off and on for two weeks in the past year. \n\nI really try to keep a good attitude, you know? Even when things get a little overwhelming at the office, I still like coming home and puttering around, or seeing my friends. I don't let myself get stuck in a rut where I just stop caring about things. I promise I'm trying to think of something to give you... is it bad that I just don't have that issue? I hope I'm still being helpful to you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
